Now, `R_step`:

```coq
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> step t t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' HR Hs.
  - simpl in HR |- *. destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + apply step_lc_preserve with t; assumption.
    + apply SN_step with t; assumption.
  - simpl in HR |- *. destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Happ]]. split; [|split].
    + apply step_lc_preserve with t; assumption.
    + apply SN_step with t; assumption.
    + intros u Hu. apply IH2. 
      * apply Happ. assumption.
      * apply ST_App1; [assumption | apply R_lc in Hu; assumption].
Qed.
```
Note: `simpl in HR |- *` — the goal R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t' unfolds similarly ✓. But careful: simpl might unfold R T1 in the hypothesis Happ too, causing the goal to become messy. Hmm, `simpl` on R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t' gives the conjunction with `forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t' u)` — R T1 stays since T1 is a variable. ✓.

But wait: in the Bool case, `simpl in HR |- *` might do nothing (already fine).

Then `step_lc_preserve`:
```coq
Lemma step_lc_preserve : forall t t', step t t' -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hs. induction Hs as [T body v Hlcabs Hv | t1 t1' t2 Hs IH Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv Hs IH].
  - (* ST_AppAbs *) intros Hlc. simpl in Hlc. apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_open_rec with (k := 0) (u := v).
    + apply lc_at_abs_inv in Hlcabs. assumption.
    + apply lc_at_weaken with 0; [lia | assumption].
  ...
```
Wait, the ST_AppAbs constructor: `ST_AppAbs : forall T t v, locally_closed (tm_abs T t) -> value v -> tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v`. Its conclusion is a step from the app, and the hypothesis Hlcabs is locally_closed of the abs. In the induction, we need lc of the *source* term app (abs T t) v and conclude lc of open t v.

But the premise `value v` doesn't give lc v! We need lc_at 0 v for lc_open_rec. Does value imply lc? v_abs requires lc; v_true/v_false are lc. So prove `value_lc : value v -> locally_closed v` ✓.

- ST_App1: source app t1 t2, target app t1' t2. lc source → lc t1, lc t2; IH (needs lc t1) gives lc t1'; conclude lc (app t1' t2) ✓. Note the ST_App1 constructor has hypotheses: step t1 t1' and locally_closed t2. In the induction, IH : locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t1' (since the statement is `forall t', step t t' -> lc t -> lc t'` and the step is the recursive argument). Careful with the induction structure: `induction Hs` where Hs : step t t' — the IH for the recursive premise `t1 --> t1'` would be the statement applied to that premise: IH : locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t1' ✓ (if the goal is appropriately generalized over t and t').

Now `anti_red_app`. Let me write it carefully.

```coq
Lemma anti_red_app : forall T (t u : tm),
  locally_closed (tm_app t u) ->
  strongly_normalizing (tm_app t u) ->
  (forall s, step (tm_app t u) s -> R T s) ->
  R T (tm_app t u).
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t u Hlc Hsn Hreds.
  - (* Bool *) simpl. split; assumption.
  - (* Arrow *) simpl. split; [assumption | split; [assumption |]].
    intros v Hv.
    apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u) (u := v).
    + (* lc (app (app t u) v) *) simpl. apply lc_app.
      * assumption.  (* Hlc : lc (app t u) *)
      * apply R_lc in Hv. assumption.
    + (* SN (app (app t u) v) *) apply SN_intro. intros s Hs.
      (* analyze step of (app (app t u) v) *)
      destruct (step_app_cases _ _ _ Hs) as [[T' [body [E1 [E2 [E3 E4]]]]] | [[s1 [Hs1 [Hlcv E]]] | [s2 [Hv' [Hs2 E]]]]].
      ...
```
Hmm, I need `step_app_cases`:
```coq
Lemma step_app_cases : forall t1 t2 s,
  step (tm_app t1 t2) s ->
  (exists T body, t1 = tm_abs T body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T body) /\ value t2 /\ s = open body t2)
  \/ (exists t1', step t1 t1' /\ locally_closed t2 /\ s = tm_app t1' t2)
  \/ (exists t2', value t1 /\ step t2 t2' /\ s = tm_app t1 t2').
Proof.
  intros t1 t2 s Hs. inversion Hs as [T body v Hlc Hv | t1' t2' Hstep Hlc2 | v1 t2' Hv Hstep]; subst.
  - left. eexists. eexists. repeat split; eauto.
    Hmm, unification: the app in the conclusion is tm_app (tm_abs T body) v, so t1 = tm_abs T body, t2 = v ✓.
  - right; left. eexists; repeat split; eauto.
  - right; right. eexists; repeat split; eauto.
Qed.
```
Wait, the ST_App1 constructor: `forall t1 t1' t2, t1 --> t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> tm_app t1 t2 --> tm_app t1' t2`. The `as` pattern: `[T body v Hlc Hv | t1_1 t1_2 t2_1 Hstep Hlc2 | v1 t2_1 t2_2 Hv Hstep]`. Since the names may clash, I'll use underscores or unique names.

Hmm, inversion with dependent variables t1 t2 being *variables* (not constructors) should be fine: the unification `tm_app t1 t2 = tm_app (tm_abs T body) v` fails for the first constructor (since tm_app ≠ tm_abs), leaving the other two. For the second: t1 = t1', t2 = t2' ✓.

Actually, constructing these inversion lemmas by hand can be error-prone. Let me consider just using `inversion Hs` directly in each proof, with `subst`, and handle the resulting goals. Sometimes inversion leaves impossible cases as goals with contradictory hypotheses (e.g., `H1 : tm_app t1 t2 = tm_abs T body`), which `discriminate` solves.

Note the ordering in `SN_ind'` refine: `SN_ind' P` has type `(forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t) -> forall t, SN t -> P t`. The goal after `intros ... Hbody` is `forall u, SN u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (...)`. Define P := fun u => R T1 u -> R T2 (...). Then `SN_ind' P` : (forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t) -> forall t, SN t -> P t. Applying it to the goal `forall u, SN u -> P u` leaves the subgoal `forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t` ✓. So `refine (SN_ind' (fun u => ...) _)` should work if the goal is exactly `forall u, SN u -> P u`. But after `intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody`, the goal is `forall u, SN u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (...)` which is definitionally `forall u, SN u -> P u` ✓. So `refine (SN_ind' P _)` where P is the explicit function. Coq should accept.

Then the subgoal: `forall u, (forall t', step u t' -> P t') -> P u`, i.e., after `intros u IH HR`: IH : ∀t', step u t' -> R T1 t' -> R T2 (app (abs T1 body) t') ✓ and HR : R T1 u.

Now the fundamental theorem:

```coq
Definition ctx_sat (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)).

Lemma update_sigma_sat : forall Gamma sigma x T1 u,
  ctx_sat Gamma sigma ->
  R T1 u ->
  ctx_sat (update Gamma x T1) (update_sigma sigma x u).
Proof.
  intros Gamma sigma x T1 u [Hlc Hall] HR. split.
  - intros y. unfold update_sigma. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply R_lc in HR. assumption.
    + apply Hlc.
  - intros y T Hty. unfold update_sigma.
    destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. 
      (* Hty : update Gamma x T1 x = Some T; update Gamma x T1 x = Some T1, so Some T1 = Some T -> T = T1 *)
      unfold update in Hty. rewrite E in Hty. (* E : x =? x = true *) 
      inversion Hty; subst. apply HR.
    + apply Hall. unfold update in Hty. rewrite E in Hty. assumption.
Qed.
```
Careful: `update Gamma x T1 y = if Nat.eqb x y then Some T1 else Gamma y` (definition: `fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else Gamma y` where the update's x is the first arg). Let me re-read the supplied definition:

```coq
Definition update (Gamma : context) (x : atom) (T : ty) : context :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else Gamma y.
```
✓.

And `update_sigma sigma x u := fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y`.

```coq
Lemma subst_update_open : forall sigma x u t,
  ~ fv x t ->
```

```coq
Lemma subst_update_open : forall sigma x u t,
  ~ fv x t ->
  (forall y, locally_closed (sigma y)) ->
  locally_closed u ->
  subst (update_sigma sigma x u) (open t (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t) u.

  intros sigma x u t Hfresh Hlc Hu. unfold open.
  rewrite subst_open_rec.
  - rewrite (subst_update_same sigma x u t Hfresh).
    simpl. unfold update_sigma. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity.
  - intros y. unfold update_sigma. destruct (Nat.eqb x y); [assumption | apply Hlc].
Qed.
```

```coq
(* ============================================================
   Infrastructure for the strong normalization proof
   ============================================================ *)

From Stdlib Require Import Lia.

(* ------- basic lemmas on lc_at and open_rec ------- *)

Lemma lc_at_weaken : forall k j t, k <= j -> lc_at k t -> lc_at j t.
Proof.
  intros k j t Hle H. revert j Hle.
  induction H; intros j Hle.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; [apply IHH1 | apply IHH2]; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma lc_at_app_inv : forall k t1 t2, lc_at k (tm_app t1 t2) -> lc_at k t1 /\ lc_at k t2.
Proof. intros k t1 t2 H. inversion H; subst; auto. Qed.

Lemma lc_at_abs_inv : forall k T t, lc_at k (tm_abs T t) -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof. intros k T t H. inversion H; subst; auto. Qed.

Lemma lc_at_bvar_inv : forall k i, lc_at k (tm_bvar i) -> i < k.
Proof. intros k i H. inversion H; subst; auto. Qed.

Lemma lc_at_fvar_inv : forall k x, lc_at k (tm_fvar x).
Proof. intros. apply lc_fvar. Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma open_rec_id : forall t k u, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t.
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k u Hlc; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. pose proof (lc_at_bvar_inv k k Hlc). lia.
    + reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2]. rewrite (IH1 k u H1), (IH2 k u H2). reflexivity.
  - apply lc_at_abs_inv in Hlc. rewrite (IH (S k) u Hlc). reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

`lc_open_rec`:
```coq
Lemma lc_open_rec : forall t k u, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k u Ht Hu; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. assumption.
    + apply lc_bvar. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. pose proof (lc_at_bvar_inv (S k) n Ht). lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app. apply IH1; assumption. apply IH2; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH with (k := S k).
    + apply lc_at_abs_inv in Ht. assumption.
    + apply lc_at_weaken with (k := k); [lia | assumption].
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```
Check the bvar case: Ht : lc_at (S k) (bvar n) → n < S k, i.e., n <= k. If k = n, result u and Hu : lc_at k u ✓ (with k = n). If k ≠ n, then n < k (from n <= k and n ≠ k) ✓.

`lc_open_inv`:
```coq
Lemma lc_open_inv : forall t k j u, j <= k -> lc_at k (open_rec j u t) -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k j u Hjk H; simpl in H.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb j n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. apply lc_bvar. lia.
    + apply lc_bvar. apply lc_at_bvar_inv in H. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_at_app_inv in H as [H1 H2]. apply lc_app. apply IH1 with (k := k) (j := j); assumption. apply IH2 with (k := k) (j := j); assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH with (k := S k) (j := S j).
    + lia.
    + apply lc_at_abs_inv in H. assumption.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.
```
In the bvar case with eqb true: subst n := j; H : lc_at k u; goal lc_at (S k) (bvar j): j < S k from j <= k ✓.
With eqb false: H : lc_at k (bvar n) → n < k → n < S k ✓.

Hmm: `apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst.` — E : (j =? n) = true; Nat.eqb_eq : forall n m, (n =? m) = true <-> n = m. So E gives j = n; subst n (replacing n by j) ✓.

Now the subst section:

```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition ids : atom -> tm := fun x => tm_fvar x.

Definition update_sigma (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Fixpoint fv (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => False
  | tm_fvar y => x = y
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv x t1 \/ fv x t2
  | tm_abs T t1 => fv x t1
  | tm_true => False
  | tm_false => False
  end.
```

Hmm, `fv` is a Fixpoint returning Prop with x as first arg fixed. For induction on t with fv x t, fine.

```coq
Lemma subst_ids : forall t, subst ids t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl; f_equal; auto.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `f_equal; auto` might not close the abs case: goal `tm_abs t (subst ids t0) = tm_abs t t0`; f_equal reduces to subst ids t0 = t0 = IHt ✓ auto.

For the app case: `tm_app (subst ids t1) (subst ids t2) = tm_app t1 t2`; f_equal gives two goals ✓ auto.

Now fresh:
```coq
Fixpoint fv_list (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => nil
  | tm_fvar y => y :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv_list t1 ++ fv_list t2
  | tm_abs T t1 => fv_list t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Lemma fv_in_fv_list : forall t x, fv x t -> In x (fv_list t).
Proof.
  induction t; simpl; intros x H; auto.
  - (* fvar *) subst. left. reflexivity.  (* H : x = a *)
  - (* app *) destruct H as [H|H]; [apply in_or_app; left | apply in_or_app; right]; auto.
  - (* abs *) auto.
Qed.
```
Hmm, the fvar case: t = tm_fvar a, H : x = a; goal In x (a :: nil) → left; subst; reflexivity? Actually `simpl` gives goal `In x (a :: nil)`. `destruct H` (x = a) → subst x or a. Let me write `subst; simpl; auto.` Let me handle case by case with explicit as-patterns to know the names:
```coq
Lemma fv_in_fv_list : forall t x, fv x t -> In x (fv_list t).
Proof.
  induction t as [n | y | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros x H; simpl in *.
  - destruct H.
  - destruct H. subst. left. reflexivity.
  - destruct H as [H | H]; [apply in_or_app; left | apply in_or_app; right]; auto.
  - apply IH. assumption.
  - destruct H.
  - destruct H.
Qed.
```

```coq
Definition fresh (L : list atom) (t : tm) : atom :=
  S (fold_right Nat.add 0 (L ++ fv_list t)).

Lemma In_fold_right_add_le : forall l x, In x l -> x <= fold_right Nat.add 0 l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x H; simpl in *.
  - destruct H.
  - destruct H as [H | H]; [subst; lia | specialize (IH x H); lia].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin : forall L t, ~ In (fresh L t) L.
Proof.
  intros L t H. unfold fresh in H.
  apply in_or_app in H. apply In_fold_right_add_le in H. lia.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_not_fv : forall L t, ~ fv (fresh L t) t.
Proof.
  intros L t H. apply fv_in_fv_list in H. unfold fresh in H.
  apply in_or_app in H. apply In_fold_right_add_le in H. lia.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma subst_update_same : forall sigma x u t,

  - reflexivity.
```

```coq
Lemma subst_open_rec : forall sigma t k u,

  - reflexivity.
```

```coq
Lemma subst_update_open : forall sigma x u t,
  ~ fv x t ->
  (forall y, locally_closed (sigma y)) ->
  locally_closed u ->
```

```coq
Lemma value_lc : forall v, value v -> locally_closed v.

Lemma value_not_app : forall t1 t2, value (tm_app t1 t2) -> False.
Proof. intros t1 t2 H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma step_abs_false : forall T body s, step (tm_abs T body) s -> False.
Proof. intros T body s H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma step_true_false : forall s, step tm_true s -> False.
Proof. intros s H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma step_false_false : forall s, step tm_false s -> False.
Proof. intros s H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma value_no_step : forall v, value v -> forall v', step v v' -> False.
Proof.
  intros v Hv. induction Hv; intros v' Hs.
  - apply step_abs_false in Hs. assumption.
  - apply step_true_false in Hs. assumption.
  - apply step_false_false in Hs. assumption.
Qed.
```

`step_app_cases`:
```coq
Lemma step_app_cases : forall t1 t2 s,
  step (tm_app t1 t2) s ->
  (exists T body, t1 = tm_abs T body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T body) /\ value t2 /\ s = open body t2)
  \/ (exists t1', step t1 t1' /\ locally_closed t2 /\ s = tm_app t1' t2)
  \/ (exists t2', value t1 /\ step t2 t2' /\ s = tm_app t1 t2').
Proof.
  intros t1 t2 s Hs.
  inversion Hs as [T body v Hlc Hv | a a' b Hstep Hlc | v1 b b' Hv Hstep]; subst.
  - left. exists T, body. repeat split; assumption.  
```
Hmm, need to check the unification: In the ST_AppAbs case, the constructor's source is `tm_app (tm_abs T body) v`, and ours is `tm_app t1 t2`; inversion should unify t1 := tm_abs T body, t2 := v, s := open body v. Then the goal is `exists T body, tm_abs T body = tm_abs T body /\ lc (tm_abs T body) /\ value v /\ open body v = open body v`. `eauto` or `repeat split; assumption` should do it. But does `inversion ... as [...]` handle variables t1, t2 that appear in the goal? The goal is the disjunction. Inversion will substitute t1 and t2. Since t1, t2 are local variables (not appearing elsewhere), it's fine.

Actually, the `as` pattern for inversion lists the constructor's arguments, but does it include equalities? With inversion, Coq generates the case with the constructor's premises as hypotheses. The pattern `[T body v Hlc Hv | ...]` — hmm, but there's a subtlety: inversion may also introduce the equation between the conclusion and the target.

A cleaner approach: prove this lemma directly with `inversion Hs; subst; eauto`. Let me try:

Hmm, that's more complex. Let me just try inversion with subst and see.

`step_lc_preserve`:
```coq
Lemma step_lc_preserve : forall t t', step t t' -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hs. induction Hs as [T body v Hlcabs Hv | t1 t1' t2 Hstep IH Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv Hstep IH]; intros Hlc.
  - simpl in Hlc. apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_open_rec with (k := 0).
    + apply lc_at_abs_inv in Hlcabs. assumption.
    + apply value_lc in Hv. assumption.
  - simpl in Hlc. apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_app. apply IH. assumption. assumption.
  - simpl in Hlc. apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_app. assumption. apply IH. assumption.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `simpl in Hlc` where Hlc : locally_closed (tm_app ...) — locally_closed is a Definition; simpl may unfold it (delta reduction for definitions marked simpl? `locally_closed` is a plain Definition; `simpl` unfolds definitions only if they lead to iota reduction? Actually `simpl` does delta-reduce definitions when the result is a reducible match... `lc_at 0 (tm_app ...)` — lc_at is inductive, not a match. Hmm, `simpl` may not unfold locally_closed. Use `unfold locally_closed in Hlc` explicitly.

Hmm, but `locally_closed t` unfolds to `lc_at 0 t` ✓. I'll use `unfold locally_closed in Hlc`.

Now the induction pattern: the `as` clause for `induction Hs` where Hs : step t t' and the goal is `locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'`. Since t and t' are indices, the induction generalizes them. The constructors:
- ST_AppAbs (T t v : ...), args: T, t, v, Hlc, Hv. No recursive occurrences. Pattern: `[T body v Hlcabs Hv | ...]`.
- ST_App1: args t1, t1', t2, Hstep (recursive), Hlc2. Pattern with IH replacing Hstep: `[t1 t1' t2 IH Hlc2]`? In Coq's `induction ... as [pat | pat]`, each constructor's pattern lists names for *all* arguments, and for recursive arguments, instead of a name you give the IH name(s)? I believe the syntax is: for each argument, if it's recursive, you provide the IH name; if non-recursive, a name. E.g., `induction H as [| n IH]` for even. For a constructor with a non-recursive argument and a recursive one, like `le_S : forall n m, le n m -> le n (S m)`, `induction H as [n | n m IH]` — the recursive argument is replaced by IH, so the pattern has fewer/more? I recall `induction H as [| n m IH]` works where le_S's args are n m and the recursive premise → IH replaces it. So the pattern includes n, m, and IH (for the recursive premise).

So for ST_App1 (args t1, t1', t2, Hstep-recursive, Hlc2): pattern `t1 t1' t2 IH Hlc2` ✓.
For ST_App2 (args v1, t2, t2', Hv, Hstep-recursive): pattern `v1 t2 t2' Hv IH` ✓.
For ST_AppAbs (args T, t, v, Hlc, Hv): `T body v Hlcabs Hv` ✓.

OK.

Hmm, the induction pattern for SN. Let me first write `SN_ind'` and derive SN_step from it:

```coq
Lemma SN_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hsn Hs.
  revert t' Hs.
  induction Hsn as [t0 Hreds IH].
  intros t' Hs. apply Hreds. assumption.
```

```coq
From Stdlib Require Import Lia.

(* ---------- lc_at and open_rec lemmas ---------- *)

Lemma lc_at_weaken : forall k j t, k <= j -> lc_at k t -> lc_at j t.
Proof.
  intros k j t Hle H. revert j Hle.
  induction H; intros j Hle.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; [apply IHH1 | apply IHH2]; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
Qed.

Lemma lc_at_app_inv : forall k t1 t2, lc_at k (tm_app t1 t2) -> lc_at k t1 /\ lc_at k t2.
Proof. intros k t1 t2 H. inversion H; subst; auto. Qed.

Lemma lc_at_abs_inv : forall k T t, lc_at k (tm_abs T t) -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof. intros k T t H. inversion H; subst; auto. Qed.

Lemma lc_at_bvar_inv : forall k i, lc_at k (tm_bvar i) -> i < k.
Proof. intros k i H. inversion H; subst; auto. Qed.

Lemma open_rec_id : forall t k u, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t.
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k u Hlc; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. pose proof (lc_at_bvar_inv k k Hlc). lia.
    + reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    rewrite (IH1 k u H1), (IH2 k u H2). reflexivity.
  - apply lc_at_abs_inv in Hlc. rewrite (IH (S k) u Hlc). reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma lc_open_rec : forall t k u, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH | | ]; intros k u Ht Hu; simpl.
```

```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (H := fundamental empty t T Hty id_sigma).
  (* ctx_sat empty id_sigma *)
  assert (Hsat : ctx_sat empty id_sigma).
  { split.
    - intros x. simpl. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T' Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate. }
  specialize (H Hsat).
  rewrite tsubst_ids in H.
  apply R_sn in H. assumption.
Qed.
```
Where `tsubst_ids : forall t, tsubst id_sigma t = t`.

Right: H is `In (S (fold_right ...)) L`, not `In ... (L ++ fv_list t)`. My fresh definition: `S (fold_right Nat.add 0 (L ++ fv_list t))`. H is In x L, and I need to relate x = fresh to the sum of L ++ fv_list t. But x ∈ L does not directly give x = S(sum) being in L... Wait, my proof strategy was: to show `fresh ∉ L`, suppose fresh ∈ L. Then fresh ≤ sum(L ++ fv_list t) < S(sum) = fresh. Contradiction.

But to apply In_fold_right_add_le, I need In fresh (L ++ fv_list t), not just In fresh L. Since L ⊆ L ++ fv_list t, I can do `apply in_or_app; left; assumption`.

So:
```
Lemma fresh_notin : forall L t, ~ In (fresh L t) L.
Proof.
  intros L t H. unfold fresh in *.
  assert (Hin : In (S (fold_right Nat.add 0 (L ++ fv_list t))) (L ++ fv_list t)).
  { apply in_or_app. left. exact H. }
  apply In_fold_right_add_le in Hin. lia.
Qed.
```
And for fresh_not_fv:
```
Lemma fresh_not_fv : forall L t, ~ fv (fresh L t) t.
Proof.
  intros L t Hfv. apply fv_in_fv_list in Hfv.
  assert (Hin : In (fresh L t) (L ++ fv_list t)).
  { apply in_or_app. right. exact Hfv. }
  unfold fresh in Hin. apply In_fold_right_add_le in Hin. lia.
Qed.
```
Note: in `unfold fresh in Hin`, Hin : In (fresh L t) (L ++ fv_list t); unfolding fresh in Hin turns it into In (S (fold...)) (L ++ fv_list t) ✓.
